```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Under prompt-chain recursion, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations; stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; retrain-chain extends discriminability to $\Kempirical{=}7$ (two families; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
5	$\Kempirical$ is threshold-dependent and should not be interpreted as an absolute constant, though domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is $\theta$-invariant.
6	A fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier raises 1v2 $\auc$ to $0.678$ (vs.\ $0.621$ for LightGBM) yet leaves $\Kempirical{=}2$ intact (2v3 CI upper ${<}\,\theta$; Section~\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), confirming a representation-independent boundary for cross-family classifiers rather than a feature ceiling.
7	Future work includes systematic retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ measurement, scale trends beyond 14B, and adaptive threshold selection methods.
8
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Even frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \geq 2$ ranges 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$).
23	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
24	
25	\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}
26	C4 yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations), Python code $\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models ($\Kempirical{=}1$ for Pythia-1.4B; Section~\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).
27	The only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\Kempirical{=}1$) yields $\Kempirical{=}2$ on code and $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, confirming domain structure as the primary factor (Appendix~\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).
28	$\Kempirical$ is also representation-dependent ($K^*_{\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring preserves $\Kempirical$ for 6/8 C4 configurations but reduces Wikipedia from 2 to 1; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
29	
30	\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}
31	All main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor.
32	Retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ (two families; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) represents the upper bound; real-world mixed contamination falls between these regimes.
33	
34	\paragraph{Discriminability vs.\ filtering utility.}
35	The gap between discriminability and filtering utility is itself informative: the modest downstream effect (0.76\,pp MMLU; Section~\ref{sec:filtering}) indicates that depth-level statistical differences do not directly translate to training-signal improvement, contextualizing $\Kempirical$ as a measurement tool rather than a curation prescriptor.
36	
37	
```

```text
93	
94	Among continuation-mode models, single-feature $\auc$ rankings are highly correlated ($\rho \geq 0.85$; Table~\ref{tab:top_features}).
95	The top-3 features (\texttt{surp\_d1\_std}, \texttt{surp\_std}, \texttt{surp\_d2\_std}) are shared across all three families: autoregressive generation progressively damps surprisal variability; however, no single feature subset of size ${<}8$ reliably sustains $\Kempirical{=}2$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_subset}).
96	A feature group ablation (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_ablation}) confirms that surprisal (base) dominates on arXiv (single AUC $= 0.809$, LOO $\Delta = 0.031$); on C4 and Wikipedia, all LOO drops fall within fold variance ($\leq 0.007$), indicating the boundary signal is carried by feature combinations rather than any single indicator.
97	Leave-one-feature-out analysis identifies \texttt{freq\_entropy} as the most stable contributor across all three domains (Appendix~\ref{sec:feature_ablation}).
98	Qwen-rewrite diverges (\texttt{freq\_entropy} dominates), consistent with its poor cross-model transfer (61--63\%).
99	Within Qwen, rewrite yields 1v2 $\auc = 0.6955$ vs.\ continuation's $0.6112$ ($\Delta = +8.4$\,pp); continuation mode is therefore recommended as the canonical condition.
100	
101	%% ====================================================================
102	\subsection{Neural Probe}
103	\label{sec:neural_probe}
104	
105	Replacing the 15D features with frozen encoder hidden states (MLP probe, 5{,}000 texts per depth; Table~\ref{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}), Qwen achieves $K^*_{\text{neural}} = 3$ (2v3 $\auc = 0.6159$), while Pythia and OLMo remain at $K^*_{\text{neural}} = 2$ ($\auc_{2v3}$: 0.5963, 0.5892).
106	The Qwen-only elevation reflects self-familiarity artifacts that transfer poorly (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}); the 15D statistical features yield consistent $\Kempirical = 2$ across all families.
107	
108	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
109	\centering
110	\footnotesize
111	\captionof{table}{Neural probe pairwise AUC using frozen-encoder MLP. Each cell reports AUC for classifying depth $d$ vs.\ $d{+}1$ text. $K^*_{\text{neural}}$: maximum depth where AUC exceeds $\theta = 0.60$.}
112	\label{tab:neural_probe_crossmodel}
113	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
114	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
115	\toprule
116	\textbf{Model} & \textbf{2v3 $\auc$} & \textbf{95\% CI} & $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ \\
117	\midrule
118	Qwen-1.5B  & 0.6159 & $[0.610,\,0.622]$ & 3 \\
119	Pythia-1.4B & 0.5963 & $[0.589,\,0.603]$ & 2 \\
120	OLMo-1B    & 0.5892 & $[0.582,\,0.597]$ & 2 \\
121	\bottomrule
122	\end{tabular}}
123	\end{minipage}
124	
125	\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}
126	To test whether a stronger classifier shifts the boundary, we train a fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier on 14B-scale C4 generations (Qwen-14B; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}).
127	DeBERTa achieves higher absolute $\auc$ than LightGBM at every depth pair (1v2: $0.678$ vs.\ $0.621$), yet $\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (1v2 CI $[0.670, 0.687]$; 2v3 CI upper $= 0.598 < \theta$).
128	The boundary is representation-independent for cross-family classifiers: contextualized embeddings improve absolute discriminability but do not extend the discrimination depth.
129	
130	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
131	\centering
132	\footnotesize
133	\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\ LightGBM on C4 14B-scale generations. DeBERTa improves absolute $\auc$ but preserves $\Kempirical{=}2$.}
134	\label{tab:deberta_comparison}
135	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
136	\toprule
137	& \textbf{0v1} & \textbf{1v2} & \textbf{2v3} & \textbf{3v4} \\
138	\midrule
139	LightGBM  & .960 & .621 & .572 & .537 \\
140	DeBERTa   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 \\
141	\bottomrule
142	\end{tabular}
143	\end{minipage}
144	
145	%% ====================================================================
146	\subsection{Cross-Domain Variation}
147	\label{sec:cross_domain}
148	
149	The preceding experiments use C4 (general web text).
150	To test whether $\Kempirical$ reflects domain structure or model identity, we evaluate on arXiv abstracts ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model) and Wikipedia ($n{=}5{,}000$ per model) across six model configurations spanning four architecture families (Table~\ref{tab:domain_kstar}).
151	
152	\paragraph{arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$): five-model cross-validation.}
153	All five domain-competent models yield $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, with boundary-pair $\auc$ (2v3) ranging from 0.6215 to 0.6545, all exceeding $\theta$.
154	The elevated signal reflects higher per-generation distributional shift (1v2 JSD $= 0.077$ vs.\ ${\sim}0.01$ for C4): formulaic features (LaTeX formatting, citation patterns, mathematical notation) degrade slowly under autoregressive generation and supply persistent depth signal across four architecture families and 1.4B--8B parameters.
155	Removing LaTeX markup reduces 2v3 AUC by ${\sim}$1.8\,pp but preserves $\Kempirical{=}3$ (CI lower $= 0.610 > \theta$; Table~\ref{tab:latex_ablation}); content-level properties dominate.
156	OLMo-1B is a capacity failure (0v1 JSD $= 0.33$, near-total information loss), not a counter-example.
157	Mistral-7B provides the strongest domain-dependence evidence: $\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4 (Section~\ref{sec:boundary_cases}) but $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv: domain structure alone can shift $\Kempirical$ by two levels within the same model.
158	
159	\paragraph{Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$): single-generation saturation.}
160	With scorer confound controlled (Pythia-1.4B for Qwen generators; Qwen-1.5B, itself cross-family, for all others), all six models yield $\Kempirical{=}1$ on Wikipedia: 1v2 $\auc$ ranges from 0.499 (OLMo) to 0.595 (LLaMA), all below $\theta$.
161	Wikipedia represents the simplest domain tested; its encyclopedic regularity provides minimal structural signal beyond the 0v1 boundary, and a single generation pass suffices for complete saturation.
162	Same-family scoring inflates Qwen $\Kempirical$ to 2 on Wikipedia ($\Delta = +2.4$\,pp); cross-family scoring controls this bias without affecting C4 results (Appendix~\ref{sec:wiki_domain}).
163	
164	\paragraph{Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$): cross-modality confirmation.}
165	Python functions from CodeSearchNet~\cite{husain2019codesearchnet} ($n{=}5{,}000$) yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models (Qwen-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-8B, Mistral-7B; 1v2 $\auc$: 0.615--0.739; Table~\ref{tab:code_crossmodel_kstar}), but $\Kempirical{=}1$ for capacity-limited Pythia-1.4B ($\auc = 0.598$, CI upper $= 0.603$).
166	Code matches C4 despite a different modality, with the same sharp 2v3 collapse (JSD ratio $11.7$--$13.5\times$).
167	Cross-family scorer validation confirms Code $\Kempirical{=}2$ is text-intrinsic ($\Delta \leq 1.9$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{sec:code_domain}).
168	
169	\paragraph{Domain as the primary factor.}
170	With the scorer confound controlled, the domain ordering is: arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $\geq$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$) $\geq$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$).
171	Scale (1B--14B) and architecture do not alter the C4/arXiv/Wikipedia ordering; code shows model-dependent variation at the boundary, but domain structural complexity remains the primary determinant of $\Kempirical$.
172	
173	\noindent\begin{minipage}{\columnwidth}
174	\centering
175	\footnotesize
176	\captionof{table}{Domain $\times$ Model $\Kempirical$ matrix (scorer-controlled). Wikipedia values use cross-family scorer (Pythia-1.4B). $^*$OLMo arXiv: capacity failure. $^\dagger$Same-family scorer yields $\Kempirical{=}2$; controlled value shown. $^\ddagger$Borderline: Pythia CI upper $= 0.603$, Mistral CI lower $= 0.603$.}
177	\label{tab:domain_kstar}
178	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{%
179	\begin{tabular}{lcccc}
```

```text
68	\section*{Limitations}
69	
70	Our findings are bounded by:
71	(1)~\emph{Scope}: model scale $\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\% reliable $\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).
72	Based on the 1.5B$\to$14B trend (no increase in $\Kempirical$, declining 1v2 AUC), we predict $\Kempirical$ may decrease to 1 for $>$30B models; this extrapolation is unverified.
73	(2)~\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.
74	(3)~\emph{Threshold}: $\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$).
75	(4)~\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\theta$.
76	(5)~\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).
77	(6)~\emph{Segment length}: $\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.
78	(7)~\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
79	(8)~\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.
80	(9)~\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.
81	Additional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
82	
83	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
84	\section*{Ethics Statement}
85	
86	Generational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.
87	Our experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.
88	A potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\auc \geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.
89	Code and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.
90	
91	\bibliography{references}
92	
93	\input{appendix}
94	\input{appendix_extended}
```

```json
  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}\nC4 yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (8/9 configurations), Python code $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for four models ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ for Pythia-1.4B; Section~\\ref{sec:cross_domain}), arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ (five models, four families), and Wikipedia $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:wiki_domain}); the ordering correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}).\nThe only C4 exception (Mistral-7B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$) yields $\\Kempirical{=}2$ on code and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv, confirming domain structure as the primary factor (Appendix~\\ref{sec:latex_ablation}).\n$\\Kempirical$ is also representation-dependent ($K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring preserves $\\Kempirical$ for 6/8 C4 configurations but reduces Wikipedia from 2 to 1; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor.\nRetrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (two families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) represents the upper bound; real-world mixed contamination falls between these regimes.\n\n\\paragraph{Discriminability vs.\\ filtering utility.}\nThe gap between discriminability and filtering utility is itself informative: the modest downstream effect (0.76\\,pp MMLU; Section~\\ref{sec:filtering}) indicates that depth-level statistical differences do not directly translate to training-signal improvement, contextualizing $\\Kempirical$ as a measurement tool rather than a curation prescriptor.",
```

```json
    "oldString": "\\paragraph{Summary.}\nThree patterns are consistent across configurations: (1)~$\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text holds across five architecture families (1B--14B) and four decoding strategies, with generation mode as the largest within-family factor ($\\Delta = 8.4$\\,pp); (2)~domain structure is the primary determinant of $\\Kempirical$, yielding the $\\theta$-invariant ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4/Code ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$); (3)~the 15D features enable largely generator-independent transfer (80--92\\% at $\\leq$1.5B, 96--100\\% at 7B), though boundary-level $\\Kempirical$ assignments remain fragile under cross-model scoring.",
    "newString": "In summary, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text is consistent across five architecture families, four decoding strategies, and two classifier representations (LightGBM, DeBERTa); the $\\theta$-invariant domain ordering arXiv $>$ C4/Code $>$ Wikipedia is driven by structural complexity rather than model identity.",

          " Binary detectors (DivEye~\\cite{basani2026diveye}, Binoculars~\\cite{hans2024binoculars}) show moderate rank correlations with depth ($|\\rho| = 0.06$--$0.46$; Table~\\ref{tab:s9_binary_spearman}) but fail as depth proxies: 6-class accuracy is at or below chance ($\\leq 0.199$ vs.\\ 0.167 baseline), pairwise $\\auc$ yields $K^*_{\\text{binary}} \\leq 1$, and multiple conditions exhibit non-monotonic AUC at deeper pairs.",
          " The observed correlation is an artifact of 0v1 separation rather than fine-grained depth signal.",
          " ",
          "-\\paragraph{Summary.}",
```

```json
  "old_string": "Under prompt-chain recursion, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations; stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; retrain-chain extends discriminability to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (two families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\n$\\Kempirical$ is threshold-dependent and should not be interpreted as an absolute constant, though domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is $\\theta$-invariant.\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier raises 1v2 $\\auc$ to $0.678$ (vs.\\ $0.621$ for LightGBM) yet leaves $\\Kempirical{=}2$ intact (2v3 CI upper ${<}\\,\\theta$; Section~\\ref{sec:kstar_boundary}), confirming a representation-independent boundary for cross-family classifiers rather than a feature ceiling.\nFuture work includes systematic retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ measurement, scale trends beyond 14B, and adaptive threshold selection methods.",
  "new_string": "Under prompt-chain recursion, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations; stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; retrain-chain extends to $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).\nThe domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is $\\theta$-invariant.\nA fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier raises 1v2 $\\auc$ to $0.678$ yet preserves $\\Kempirical{=}2$ (2v3 CI upper ${<}\\,\\theta$), confirming a representation-independent boundary rather than a feature ceiling.\nFuture work includes retrain-chain $\\Kempirical$ measurement, scale trends beyond 14B, and adaptive threshold selection."
```

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Even frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \geq 2$ ranges 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$).
23	The retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
24	
25	\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}
26	The domain ordering arXiv ($\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\Kempirical{=}2$) $\geq$ Code ($\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\Kempirical{=}1$) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4, $\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv) confirms domain as the primary factor over architecture.
27	$\Kempirical$ is also representation-dependent ($K^*_{\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo; Section~\ref{sec:neural_probe}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring preserves $\Kempirical$ for 6/8 C4 configurations; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
28	
29	\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}
30	All main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor, with retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) as the upper bound.
31	
32	
```

Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nEven frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \\geq 2$ ranges 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix~\\ref{sec:frontier_probe}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$).\nThe retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) reflects cumulative model-adaptation artifacts, extending $\\Kempirical$ to 7 (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}\nThe domain ordering arXiv ($\\Kempirical{=}3$) $>$ C4 ($\\Kempirical{=}2$) $\\geq$ Code ($\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$) $>$ Wikipedia ($\\Kempirical{=}1$) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}); Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$ on C4, $\\Kempirical{=}3$ on arXiv) confirms domain as the primary factor over architecture.\n$\\Kempirical$ is also representation-dependent ($K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo; Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}) and scorer-dependent (cross-family scoring preserves $\\Kempirical$ for 6/8 C4 configurations; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor, with retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) as the upper bound.\n\n",

```json
    "originalFile": "\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\\label{sec:why_kstar}\n\\label{sec:one_wash}\n\\label{sec:negative_graduated}\n\\label{sec:repr_dependence}\n\\label{sec:boundary_cases}\n\\label{sec:length_confound}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}\n\\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\\lambda \\approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\\lambda = 0.285$).}\n\\label{fig:jsd_decay}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}\nFor general web text, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\\auc \\geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\\sim}$30\\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.\nEven frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \\geq 2$ ranges 8--38\\% (vs.\\ 20\\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix~\\ref{sec:frontier_probe}).\n\n\\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}\nToken-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); the retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) extends $\\Kempirical$ to 7 via cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).\n\n\\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}\nThe domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}) and is robust to classifier representation ($K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo) and scorer choice (6/8 C4 configurations preserved under cross-family scoring; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).\n\n\\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}\nAll main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor, with retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) as the upper bound.\n\n",
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01QqDjpWbcSe6VRcAwpRfs7R`</summary>

```text
1	\section{Discussion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	\label{sec:why_kstar}
4	\label{sec:one_wash}
5	\label{sec:negative_graduated}
6	\label{sec:repr_dependence}
7	\label{sec:boundary_cases}
8	\label{sec:length_confound}
9	
10	\begin{figure}[t]
11	\centering
12	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_jsd_decay.pdf}
13	\caption{Token-level JSD between adjacent depth pairs on log scale. Blue band: min-max range across 8 C4 models (1B--14B) with $\Kempirical{=}2$; dashed purple: Mistral-7B ($\Kempirical{=}1$, cross-scorer validated); green: arXiv Pythia ($\Kempirical{=}3$); dotted red: retrain-chain ($\Kempirical{=}7$). C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff ($\lambda \approx 2.2$--$3.5$), while arXiv decays gradually ($\lambda \approx 1.0$) and retrain-chain shows no cliff ($\lambda = 0.285$).}
14	\label{fig:jsd_decay}
15	\end{figure}
16	
17	\paragraph{One-wash laundering (web text).}
18	For general web text, $\Kempirical{=}2$ reflects concentrated information loss: 0v1 is near-trivially separable ($\auc \geq 0.92$), 1v2 drops by ${\sim}$30\,pp, and 2v3 falls below $\theta$, so a single generation pass renders text indistinguishable from deeper content.
19	Even frontier LLMs exhibit comparable collapse beyond $\Kempirical$: per-depth recall at $d \geq 2$ ranges 8--38\% (vs.\ 20\% chance), indicating error redistribution rather than genuine depth recovery (Appendix).
20	
21	\paragraph{JSD decay and the discrimination boundary.}
22	Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\sim}$3$\times$ slower ($\lambda \approx 1.0$ vs.\ $2.9$); the retrain-chain ($\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) extends $\Kempirical$ to 7 via cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (Appendix~\ref{sec:jsd_extended}).
23	
24	\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}
25	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}) and is robust to classifier representation ($K^*_{\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo) and scorer choice (6/8 C4 configurations preserved under cross-family scoring; Section~\ref{sec:transfer}).
26	
27	\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}
28	All main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor, with retrain-chain $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) as the upper bound.
29	
30	
```

```json
          " Token-level JSD between adjacent generations reveals two decay regimes (Figure~\\ref{fig:jsd_decay}): C4 models exhibit a sharp cliff (0v1 JSD exceeds 1v2 by 6--56$\\times$) followed by rapid convergence, while arXiv decays ${\\sim}$3$\\times$ slower ($\\lambda \\approx 1.0$ vs.\\ $2.9$); the retrain-chain ($\\lambda = 0.285$, no cliff) extends $\\Kempirical$ to 7 via cumulative model-adaptation artifacts (Appendix~\\ref{sec:jsd_extended})."
        ]
      },
      {
        "oldStart": 24,
        "oldLines": 6,
        "newStart": 25,
        "newLines": 3,
        "lines": [
          " \\paragraph{Domain and representation dependence.}",
          " The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) correlates with structural complexity (Table~\\ref{tab:domain-ttr-entropy}) and is robust to classifier representation ($K^*_{\\text{neural}} = 3$ for Qwen vs.\\ $2$ for Pythia/OLMo) and scorer choice (6/8 C4 configurations preserved under cross-family scoring; Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}).",
          " ",
          "-\\paragraph{Prompt-chain as detection floor.}",
          "-All main results use prompt-chain, which produces fewer distributional artifacts than retrain-chain; $\\Kempirical{=}2$ therefore establishes a detection floor, with retrain-chain $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}) as the upper bound.",
          "-"
        ]
      }
    ],

  },
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Under prompt-chain recursion, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations; stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; retrain-chain extends to $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
5	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is $\theta$-invariant.
6	A fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier raises 1v2 $\auc$ to $0.678$ yet preserves $\Kempirical{=}2$ (2v3 CI upper ${<}\,\theta$), confirming a representation-independent boundary rather than a feature ceiling.
7	Future work includes retrain-chain $\Kempirical$ measurement, scale trends beyond 14B, and adaptive threshold selection.
8	
```

For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo).
```text
68	\section*{Limitations}
69	
70	Our findings are bounded by:
71	(1)~\emph{Scope}: model scale $\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\% reliable $\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\ref{sec:power_curve}).
72	Based on the 1.5B$\to$14B trend (no increase in $\Kempirical$, declining 1v2 AUC), we predict $\Kempirical$ may decrease to 1 for $>$30B models; this extrapolation is unverified.
73	(2)~\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.
74	(3)~\emph{Threshold}: $\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\Kempirical{=}1$).
75	(4)~\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\theta$.
76	(5)~\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).
77	(6)~\emph{Segment length}: $\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.
78	(7)~\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\%; Appendix~\ref{sec:dose_response}).
79	(8)~\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.
80	(9)~\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.
81	Additional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.
82	
83	% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===
```

```json
      "content": "\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: model scale $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nBased on the 1.5B$\\to$14B trend (no increase in $\\Kempirical$, declining 1v2 AUC), we predict $\\Kempirical$ may decrease to 1 for $>$30B models; this extrapolation is unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===",
      "numLines": 16,
      "startLine": 68,
      "totalLines": 97
    }
  },
```

```text
125	\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}
126	To test whether a stronger classifier shifts the boundary, we train a fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier on 14B-scale C4 generations (Qwen-14B; Table~\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}).
127	DeBERTa achieves higher absolute $\auc$ than LightGBM at every depth pair (1v2: $0.678$ vs.\ $0.621$), yet $\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (1v2 CI $[0.670, 0.687]$; 2v3 CI upper $= 0.598 < \theta$).
128	The boundary is representation-independent for cross-family classifiers: contextualized embeddings improve absolute discriminability but do not extend the discrimination depth.
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "\\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}\nTo test whether a stronger classifier shifts the boundary, we train a fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifier on 14B-scale C4 generations (Qwen-14B; Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}).\nDeBERTa achieves higher absolute $\\auc$ than LightGBM at every depth pair (1v2: $0.678$ vs.\\ $0.621$), yet $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (1v2 CI $[0.670, 0.687]$; 2v3 CI upper $= 0.598 < \\theta$).\nThe boundary is representation-independent for cross-family classifiers: contextualized embeddings improve absolute discriminability but do not extend the discrimination depth.\n\n\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ LightGBM on C4 14B-scale generations. DeBERTa improves absolute $\\auc$ but preserves $\\Kempirical{=}2$.}\n\\label{tab:deberta_comparison}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\textbf{0v1} & \\textbf{1v2} & \\textbf{2v3} & \\textbf{3v4} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM  & .960 & .621 & .572 & .537 \\\\\nDeBERTa   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{minipage}",
  "new_string": "\\paragraph{Fine-tuned DeBERTa upper bound.}\nTo test whether classifier strength or generator scale shifts the boundary, we train DeBERTa-v3-base ordinal classifiers on C4 generations at 14B and 32B scales (Table~\\ref{tab:deberta_comparison}).\nDeBERTa raises 1v2 $\\auc$ over LightGBM at both scales (14B: $0.678$ vs.\\ $0.621$; 32B: $0.694$ vs.\\ $0.618$), yet $\\Kempirical{=}2$ is preserved (all 2v3 CI upper ${<}\\,\\theta$).\nExtending to 32B confirms scale invariance: LightGBM $\\auc$ shifts by ${<}\\,0.5$\\,pp between 14B and 32B, and the boundary is representation-independent across both scales.\n\n\\noindent\\begin{minipage}{\\columnwidth}\n\\centering\n\\footnotesize\n\\captionof{table}{DeBERTa-v3-base vs.\\ LightGBM on C4 generations at two scales. Both classifiers yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at 14B and 32B.}\n\\label{tab:deberta_comparison}\n\\begin{tabular}{lcccc}\n\\toprule\n& \\textbf{0v1} & \\textbf{1v2} & \\textbf{2v3} & \\textbf{3v4} \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (14B)  & .960 & .621 & .572 & .537 \\\\\nDeBERTa (14B)   & .994 & .678 & .590 & .565 \\\\\n\\midrule\nLightGBM (32B)  & .958 & .618 & .545 & ---  \\\\\nDeBERTa (32B)   & .993 & .694 & .594 & ---  \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{minipage}"
}
```

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "(1)~\\emph{Scope}: model scale $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nBased on the 1.5B$\\to$14B trend (no increase in $\\Kempirical$, declining 1v2 AUC), we predict $\\Kempirical$ may decrease to 1 for $>$30B models; this extrapolation is unverified.",
  "new_string": "(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 9-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified."
}
```

```json
    "newString": "(1)~\\emph{Scope}: full 9-configuration characterization at $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), with 32B-scale validation via LightGBM and DeBERTa (Section~\\ref{sec:neural_probe}); English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nThe 1.5B$\\to$32B trend shows stable $\\Kempirical{=}2$ with declining 1v2 AUC; whether this holds at 70B+ scales remains unverified.",
    "originalFile": "% EMNLP 2026 Main - ARR May 2026\n% Generational Depth Contamination\n\\documentclass[11pt]{article}\n\\usepackage[review]{acl}\n\\usepackage{times}\n\\usepackage{latexsym}\n\\usepackage[T1]{fontenc}\n\\usepackage[utf8]{inputenc}\n\\usepackage{microtype}\n\\usepackage{graphicx}\n\\usepackage{amsmath}\n\\usepackage{amssymb}\n\\usepackage{booktabs}\n\\usepackage{multirow}\n\\usepackage{enumitem}\n\\usepackage{subcaption}\n\\usepackage{pifont}\n\\newcommand{\\cmark}{\\ding{51}}\n\\newcommand{\\xmark}{\\ding{55}}\n\\usepackage{placeins}\n\n\\newcommand{\\Kstar}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Kempirical}{{K^*}}\n\\newcommand{\\Ktheory}{{K^*_{\\text{theory}}}}\n\\newcommand{\\auc}{\\text{AUC}}\n\\newcommand{\\jsd}{\\text{JSD}}\n\\newcommand{\\tpm}{\\ensuremath{\\,\\pm\\,}}\n\n\\title{Generational Depth Estimation of Recursive Synthetic Text:\\\\Measuring the Discrimination Boundary}\n\n\\author{Anonymous}\n\n\\begin{document}\n\\maketitle\n\n% === Abstract ===\n\\begin{abstract}\nWeb-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.\nWe measure the discrimination boundary $\\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.\nAcross five architecture families (1B--14B), four source domains, and four decoding strategies, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (stable across $\\theta \\in [0.575, 0.625]$; 8/9 configurations), $\\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv.\nA single generation pass renders synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\\emph{one-wash laundering}).\nLargely generator-independent features enable 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B and 96--100\\% mean AUC retention at 7B across tested continuation-mode generators, though boundary-level transfer remains fragile; cross-scorer validation confirms $\\Kempirical$ robustness for non-borderline configurations (20/22).\nAs a consistency check, $\\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for web text, though with limited effect sizes (0.76\\,pp MMLU).\n\\end{abstract}\n\n% === Section 1: Introduction ===\n\\input{introduction}\n\n% === Section 2: Related Work ===\n\\input{related_work}\n\n% === Section 3: Method ===\n\\input{method}\n\n% === Section 4: Experiments ===\n\\input{experiments_c1}\n\n% === Section 5: K*-Aware Data Filtering ===\n\\input{experiments_c3}\n\n% === Section 6: Discussion ===\n\\input{discussion}\n\n% === Section 7: Conclusion ===\n\\input{conclusion}\n\n% === Limitations (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Limitations}\n\nOur findings are bounded by:\n(1)~\\emph{Scope}: model scale $\\leq$14B (5 architecture families), English text (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv) and Python code (CodeSearchNet), four decoding strategies; arXiv $\\Kempirical{=}3$ is confirmed with five models across four architecture families but with reduced sample size ($n{=}2{,}200$ per model); code domain tested with five models across four families (Qwen2.5-1.5B/7B, LLaMA-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B-v0.3, $\\Kempirical{=}2$; Pythia-1.4B, $\\Kempirical{=}1$ with 1v2 CI upper bound $> \\theta$); subsampling analysis indicates $n{\\geq}2{,}000$ suffices for 100\\% reliable $\\Kempirical$ determination (Appendix~\\ref{sec:power_curve}).\nBased on the 1.5B$\\to$14B trend (no increase in $\\Kempirical$, declining 1v2 AUC), we predict $\\Kempirical$ may decrease to 1 for $>$30B models; this extrapolation is unverified.\n(2)~\\emph{Protocol}: all main experiments use prompt-chain, which isolates text-intrinsic degradation from training dynamics; real-world contamination involves both prompting and retraining, and retrain-chain yields $\\Kempirical{=}7$ (both Qwen-1.5B and Pythia-1.4B LoRA; Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}), so prompt-chain $\\Kempirical$ may underestimate discriminability in mixed settings.\n(3)~\\emph{Threshold}: $\\Kempirical$ ranges from 2--3 at $\\theta{=}0.55$ to 1 at $\\theta{=}0.70$; 8/9 configurations yield $\\Kempirical{=}2$ at $\\theta{=}0.60$ (Mistral-7B: $\\Kempirical{=}1$).\n(4)~\\emph{Marginal boundary}: Qwen-continuation's 1v2 CI lower bound exceeds $\\theta$ by only 0.002; cross-scorer validation drops this to $\\Kempirical{=}1$ (Section~\\ref{sec:transfer}). LLaMA-3.1-8B (C4) and Mistral-7B-v0.3 (code) represent the most marginal $\\Kempirical{=}2$ assignments, with 1v2 CI lower bounds within 0.3 percentage points of $\\theta$.\n(5)~\\emph{Feature set}: the 15D features yield a feature-set-specific lower bound ($\\Ktheory$ may be strictly higher); $K^*_{\\text{neural}}$ is model-dependent ($3$ for Qwen, $2$ for Pythia/OLMo).\n(6)~\\emph{Segment length}: $\\Kempirical{=}2$ confirmed for 128 and 256 tokens (CI lower $> \\theta$); 512 tokens yields point estimate $\\Kempirical{=}2$ but CI lower marginally below $\\theta$ ($0.5979$); the boundary weakens at longer generation lengths.\n(7)~\\emph{Curation applicability}: the filtering evaluation is validated on two architectures (Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct and Pythia-1.4B) with consistent strategy ordering (Appendix~\\ref{sec:pythia_filtering}), though absolute improvement remains small and effect sizes diminish at lower contamination ratios (10--50\\%; Appendix~\\ref{sec:dose_response}).\n(8)~\\emph{Family balance}: the Qwen2.5 family provides 5/9 configurations; though spanning three scales (1.5B, 7B, 14B) and two training variants (base, instruct), aggregate statistics weight this family more heavily than the four single-configuration families.\n(9)~\\emph{Transfer coverage}: cross-model transfer validation shows 80--92\\% accuracy retention at $\\leq$1.5B (4 configurations) and 96--100\\% mean pairwise AUC retention at 7B (3 models, 3 architecture families; Appendix~\\ref{sec:7b_transfer}); however, at the $\\Kempirical$ boundary (1v2), 4/6 off-diagonal 7B pairs fall below $\\theta$, indicating that boundary-level transfer remains fragile even as aggregate transfer strengthens with scale.\nAdditional limitations (cross-model scoring sensitivity, feature ranking variability at the 1v2 boundary, structural composition confound analysis) appear in Appendix~\\ref{sec:extended_limitations}.\n\n% === Ethics Statement (ACL required, unnumbered) ===\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nGenerational depth estimation is intended for data curation and corpus auditing, positive-sum applications that improve training data quality.\nOur experiments use publicly available corpora (C4, Wikipedia, arXiv abstracts) and do not involve personally identifiable information.\nA potential dual-use concern is that depth estimation techniques could inform adversarial strategies to evade detection (e.g., by calibrating rewriting to mimic earlier-generation statistics); we note that this risk is shared by all detection research and that the binary detection boundary ($\\auc \\geq 0.96$ at 0v1) remains robust.\nCode and generated corpora will be released upon acceptance to enable reproducibility.\n\n\\bibliography{references}\n\n\\input{appendix}\n\\input{appendix_extended}\n\n\\end{document}\n",
```

```text
1	\section{Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:conclusion}
3	
4	Under prompt-chain recursion, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\theta{=}0.60$ (8/9 configurations; stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv; retrain-chain extends to $\Kempirical{=}7$ (Appendix~\ref{sec:extended_limitations}).
5	The domain ordering (arXiv $>$ C4 $\geq$ Code $>$ Wikipedia) is $\theta$-invariant.
6	A fine-tuned DeBERTa-v3-base classifier raises 1v2 $\auc$ to $0.678$ yet preserves $\Kempirical{=}2$ (2v3 CI upper ${<}\,\theta$), confirming a representation-independent boundary; future work includes retrain-chain measurement and scale trends beyond 14B.
7	
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YRfuAAs64F1pnxCC59kBfU`</summary>

```text
37	\begin{abstract}
38	Web-scale corpora are increasingly contaminated by recursive synthetic content, yet no method estimates a document's generational depth.
39	We measure the discrimination boundary $\Kempirical$ under prompt-chain generation using 15 statistical features and an OBD-LightGBM classifier.
40	Across five architecture families (1B--14B), four source domains, and four decoding strategies, $\Kempirical{=}2$ for web text at $\theta{=}0.60$ (stable across $\theta \in [0.575, 0.625]$; 8/9 configurations), $\Kempirical{=}1$--$2$ for code, and $\Kempirical{=}3$ for arXiv.
41	A single generation pass renders synthetic web text statistically indistinguishable from deeper generations (\emph{one-wash laundering}).
42	Largely generator-independent features enable 80--92\% accuracy retention at $\leq$1.5B and 96--100\% mean AUC retention at 7B across tested continuation-mode generators, though boundary-level transfer remains fragile; cross-scorer validation confirms $\Kempirical$ robustness for non-borderline configurations (20/22).
43	As a consistency check, $\Kempirical{=}2$ correctly predicts binary $>$ graduated filtering for web text, though with limited effect sizes (0.76\,pp MMLU).
44	\end{abstract}
```
